金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 13 日
上週重點訊息
◼ TOPIX 啟動大規模改革：日本交易所集團 10 月 7 日公布 TOPIX 調整結果，符合新制納入標
準的成分股將由 9 月底的 1,636 檔降至 986 檔，另有 35 檔股票新納入。現有成分股中，共
有 683 檔未符合流動性及浮動市值等標準，這些股票將自 2026 年 10 月底起逐步降低權
重，並於 2027 年 10 月重新評估，屆時仍未達標者將於 2028 年 7 月底降至零。此次改革有
助於改善 TOPIX 成分股的流動性及投資價值，並促使企業更加重視市場評價。

◼ 巴西總統選舉波索納洛首輪領先魯拉：巴西最高選舉法院 10 月 5 日公布首輪投票結果，參
議員弗拉維奧・波索納洛取得 47.03%的有效票，現任總統魯拉獲得 45.16%，兩人均未取得
過半票數，將於 10 月 25 日進行第二輪決選。若最終由波索納洛當選將相較有利於巴西股市
表現。
◼ IEA 支持加速釋出既有儲油：國際能源署（IEA）10 月 7 日表示，會員國支持加快釋出 3 月
已承諾提供的儲油，並將優先增加柴油供應。截至目前，會員國已釋出約 3.25 億桶儲油；若
剩餘承諾全數執行，可再向市場提供約 1 億桶供應，有助緩解運輸業與農業面臨的燃料成本
壓力。隨各國持續釋出戰略儲備石油，將有利於在短期內壓抑全球油價進一步上行。
市場展望
◼ 標普 500 獲利預估連續上修，資訊科技為主要推升來源：標普 500 企業獲利預估連續兩季上
修，其中資訊科技產業為主要改善來源，反映 AI、雲端運算及資料中心需求仍然強勁。雖然
美國資料中心仍面臨電力與基礎設施瓶頸，但建置規模持續擴大，AI 投資也逐步延伸至記憶

體、網路設備、資安及企業應用等領域。因此，預期科技類股的獲利動能仍具支撐，具 AI 供
應鏈優勢的台股與日股亦可望延續相關成長動能。
◼ 中天期債券可望兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府與企業發債供給維持高
檔，可能使市場利率維持在較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企業獲利、
現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，較高的美國公債基準利率也使其利息收益維持一定水準。短天
期債券面臨利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較易受到公債供給及利率波動影響，因
此中天期美國公債及投資級債可望在息收與利率風險之間維持較為平衡的表現。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
經濟 
⚫ 2026 年第 2 季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值上修至 2.2%， 惟低於第1 季
之 2.5%。成長主要由個人消費及固定投資帶動，其中消費貢獻 2.51 個
百分點；惟進口大增 12.6%，致淨出口拖累 1.10 個百分點。另實質私人
國內最終銷售成長 4.6%，顯示內需仍具韌性。 
⚫ 9 月美國非農就業僅增 2.9 萬人，遠低於前月及近一年平均，失業率升
至 4.2%， 薪資增幅續降 ， 顯示企業聘僱動能轉弱 。 惟勞動市場尚未明顯
惡化，後續仍須關注就業降溫對聯準會利率決策之影響。 
⚫ 8 月美國職位空缺數由 733.5 萬個降至 707.9 萬個，職缺率降至 4.3%，
顯示企業徵才需求略為降溫；惟聘僱人數增至 519.2 萬人。主動離職及
裁員規模同步下降，反映勞工轉職意願與企業人力調整趨於審慎，勞動
市場供需大致維持平衡。 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數為 54.5，連續第九個月維持擴張。
新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示需求仍具韌性；惟生產、出
口及進口動能略為放緩，且原物料成本持續攀升，加上關稅政策、地緣
政治及供應鏈不確定性仍存，製造業仍面臨部分壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國 PCE 物價指數月增 0.3%、年增 3.4%，核心 PCE 月增 0.2%、
年增 3.0%， 顯示通膨大致持穩但短期物價壓力略升 。 另一方面 ， 個人消
費支出月增 0.9%、實質消費成長 0.6%，帶動儲蓄率降至 4.1%，顯示家
庭需求仍具韌性；後續須關注消費熱度及通膨對聯準會政策之影響。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期聯準會官員立場趨於分歧，理事 Michael Barr 認為能源價格及 AI
投資熱潮可能延緩通膨降溫 ， 未來仍可能進一步升息 ； 惟John Williams、
Philip Jefferson 及 Michelle Bowman 等官員則主張暫無急於升息的必要 ，
傾向觀察後續經濟數據。加以 9 月非農就業明顯放緩，市場對 10 月升
息預期大幅降溫，焦點轉向 12 月會議。 
⚫ 台積電傳評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作赴德州設廠，雙方仍處討論階
段。合作可能由台積電主導營運，或由 Terafab 持多數股權、台積電提
供技術 。 若成真 ， 將擴大台積電美國布局 ， 並支援Tesla、SpaceX 及 xAI
晶片需求。

險有限 ； 惟近期新增就業明顯低於預期 ， 企業聘僱意願與職缺需求放緩 ，

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.5 
一、 美國—美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
2026 年第二季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值由前次估計之 1.5%上修至 2.2%，
惟仍低於第一季之 2.5%， 顯示經濟持續擴張但成長動能略有放緩 。 此次上修主要
反映投資、消費及政府支出優於原先估計。就成長結構觀察，個人消費支出仍為
主要動能，貢獻 2.51 個百分點，其中服務及商品消費分別貢獻 1.57 及 0.94 個百
分點；私人投資貢獻 0.82 個百分點，以設備投資及智慧財產權投資表現強勁，惟
庫存調整拖累 0.53 個百分點。對外貿易方面，出口成長 5.0%，但進口大幅增加
12.6%， 致淨出口拖累GDP 成長 1.10 個百分點 。 另反映內需基本面的實質私人國
內最終銷售成長 4.6%， 高於第一季之1.8%， 顯示民間需求明顯增強 。 物價方面 ，
第 2 季 PCE 及核心 PCE 物價指數分別上升 5.0%及 3.3%， 顯示通膨壓力仍偏高 。
整體而言，美國經濟仍由消費與固定投資支撐，惟進口快速增加及通膨黏著性，
仍為後續經濟前景的重要觀察重點。 
2026 年 9 月美國非農就業人數增加 2.9 萬人 ， 明顯低於上月修正後的13.3 萬
人，也低於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 4.5 萬人的水準；失業率小幅升至 4.2%，
整體仍維持在 3 月以來 4.1%至 4.3%的區間，反映勞動市場整體變化有限，惟新
增就業力道已轉趨疲弱。家庭調查方面，勞動參與率升至 61.8%，就業人口比為
59.2%，均較上月小幅上升；長期失業人數約 194.4 萬人，占總失業人口 27.1%，
大致維持穩定。產業別方面，主要產業就業變化有限，其中醫療保健增加 1.7 萬
人、營建業增加 1.1 萬人、製造業增加 0.9 萬人，金融活動則減少 0.7 萬人，政府
部門減少 1.7 萬人。薪資方面，私人部門平均時薪升至 37.81 美元，月增 0.1%、
年增 3.0%，平均每週工時維持 34.4 小時。另 7 月與 8 月新增就業合計下修 6 萬
人，反映前二月就業動能較初估疲弱。整體而言，9 月美國新增就業明顯放緩、
失業率小幅上升且薪資增速降溫，企業聘僱動能較先前轉弱；惟失業率仍維持於
近期區間，勞動市場尚未出現明顯惡化跡象，後續仍須關注企業聘僱需求是否持
續降溫，以及對聯準會利率決策的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國職位空缺數(Job Openings) 由上月修正後之 733.5 萬個減少
25.6 萬個至 707.9 萬個 ， 職缺率由4.4%降至 4.3%， 顯示企業用人需求略有降溫 ；

惟聘僱人數由 514.6 萬人小幅增加至 519.2 萬人，聘僱率由 3.2%升至 3.3%。總離
職人數由 512.8 萬人減少至 507.0 萬人，離職率維持於 3.2%。就離職結構觀察，
主動離職(Quits)人數由 308.9 萬人降至 306.6 萬人，主動離職率維持 1.9%；裁員
與解僱(Layoffs and Discharges) 人數則由 170.2 萬人降至 164.1 萬人，裁員率由
1.1%降至 1.0%。產業別方面，職位空缺增加主要來自零售業、金融保險業、資訊
業及休閒餐飲業 ； 聘僱人數則以政府部門及製造業增幅較為明顯 。 整體而言 ，8 月
美國職缺數較前月下滑，反映企業徵才需求略有降溫，但聘僱活動仍維持穩定，
且主動離職與裁員規模同步下降，顯示勞工轉職意願及企業人力調整趨於審慎，
勞動市場供需仍大致維持平衡。 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 為 54.5，
較上月之 54.6 微降 0.1 點，惟已連續第九個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，顯示製
造業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由53.7 升至 55.3，需求動能續增；生
產指數由 58.3 降至 56.7，雖仍維持擴張，但成長速度略有放緩；就業指數由51.2
升至 52.7，反映企業聘僱需求持續增加。供應商交貨指數由 59.3 微降至 59.0，交
貨延遲情況仍存；庫存指數則由 50.6 降至 48.6，企業備貨態度轉趨保守。此外，
價格指數由 71.1 升至 77.9，原物料及投入成本壓力升高；未完成訂單指數由51.8
升至 56.4，訂單去化變慢。另一方面，新出口訂單指數由53.2 降至 50.9，進口指
數亦由 52.5 降至 51.0，外需成長動能及企業備料需求略為放緩。整體而言，9 月
美國製造業活動持續穩健擴張，新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示製造
業景氣仍具韌性；惟出口及進口增速放緩、投入成本壓力升高，加上關稅政策、
地緣政治風險及供應鏈不確定性仍存，後續發展仍值得持續關注。 
2026 年 8 月美國個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增率由上月之 0.1%升至 0.3%，
年增率維持於 3.4%； 扣除食品及能源後之核心PCE 月增率由 0.1%升至 0.2%，年
增率亦持平於 3.0%，顯示整體通膨大致持穩，惟短期物價漲勢較前月略有回升。
另一方面，個人所得月增 0.2%，可支配所得(DPI)月增 0.3%，實質可支配所得持
平；個人消費支出月增 0.9%，實質個人消費支出成長 0.6%，顯示民間消費表現
明顯回溫。由於消費增幅高於所得增幅，個人儲蓄率由前月之 4.6%降至 4.1%。
整體而言，8 月美國通膨表現大致持穩，但消費支出顯著回升，反映家庭需求仍
具韌性；後續仍須持續關注消費熱度、通膨走勢及其對聯準會貨幣政策路徑之影
響。

近期聯準會官員談話呈現鷹鴿分歧。聯準會理事 Michael Barr 認為，中東局
勢推升能源價格，以及 AI 相關投資熱潮帶動需求增長，均可能阻礙通膨回落至
2%目標 ， 未來仍不排除進一步升息的必要性 ； 惟 紐約聯準銀行總裁 John Williams、
聯準會副主席 Philip Jefferson 及金融監理副主席 Michelle Bowman 則強調，在 9
月升息後，當前並無急迫性立即進一步收緊政策，應先觀察後續通膨、就業及金
融市場數據變化，再評估是否有進一步調整利率的必要。受官員談話轉趨審慎，
以及 9 月非農就業表現疲弱影響，市場對 10 月 FOMC 會議再度升息的預期明顯
降溫，政策焦點逐漸轉向 12 月會議及年底前是否仍有一次升息空間。 
晶圓代工龍頭台積電傳出正評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作，在美國德州布
局晶圓廠，馬斯克也於社群平台 X 證實雙方正在討論，並表示 「目前僅在討論階
段 ， 但可能會有進展」 。 目前浮上檯面的合作模式包括由台積電持有並營運新廠 ，
SpaceX 或 Terafab 透過入股及承諾採購晶圓參與；另一方案則由 Terafab 掌握多
數股權 ， 台積電提供製程技術與晶圓廠營運經驗 。Terafab 旨在因應 Tesla、SpaceX
及 xAI 快速成長的 AI 晶片需求，首期投資規模達 168 億美元。若合作成真，不
僅可望成為台積電繼亞利桑那州後在美國的新布局，也凸顯先進製程與穩定晶片
供應的重要性，但目前具體合作方式、時程與投資內容仍未正式定案。 
二、歐元區—法國殖利率大幅上升，不利於投資及消費表現 
 歐元區 9 月 HICP 通膨 、 核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別上升 0.6、0.1 個百分
點至 3.8%、2.5%， 主因國際原油價格上漲 ， 使能源通膨上升4.5 個百分點至 18.8%，
並透過肥料、物流成本上漲，間接推升食品通膨 0.3 個百分點至 1.4%。另外，服
務通膨上升 0.2 個百分點至 3.2%，近三個月服務通膨大致呈現橫向波動，部分反
映旅遊旺季因素影響。基於布蘭特原油持續位於每桶 90 美元以上，預期年底前
仍將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物流等成本管
道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
 其他經濟數據包含 ：(1)歐元區 8 月失業率持平 6.4%， 失業人數增加2.6 萬人 ，
主要是義大利勞動參與人數增加所致，不過 ，基於PMI 顯示近期德國勞動雇用需
求持續增溫，將有助於穩定勞動市場表現；(2)歐元區第二季家庭儲蓄率下降 0.2
個百分點至 14.2%，顯示通膨壓力削弱民眾購買力，致儲蓄暫時性減少；(3)歐元
區第二季房價年增率為 4%， 較第一季降0.6 個百分點 ， 主因法國中古屋面臨嚴格

環保法規，須負擔高昂翻修成本，使法國房價呈現連三季下滑所拖累。 
 近一週法國 10 年期公債殖利率升至 4.9%以上，與德國公債殖利率差距擴大
至 125 個基點以上，主要反映市場對法國主權公債可持續性疑慮升溫。儘管目前
政府計畫將 2027 年財政赤字縮限至 5%，惟政府在國會並無穩定多數席次，即使
能以行政命令強行通過預算 ， 也可能面臨倒閣風險 ；此外 ，市場對於2027 年 4 月
總統大選後，新總統能否取得國會足夠支持，推動削減赤字與控制債務的改革亦
缺乏信心。因此未來法國仍可能面臨殖利率進一步上升與財政壓力加劇的風險，
暫時不利於投資及消費信心。 
三、日本—景氣溫和復甦，但消費放緩與通膨升溫風險升高 
 8 月工業生產月減 1.7%，主要係受到該月地震及豪雨等天然災害影響，導致
部分工廠暫時停產。從細項來看，汽車業受停工影響最為明顯，產量下滑6.8%，
電子產品產量亦減少 1.9%。 惟生產機械產量連續三個月成長 ， 加上核心資本財出
貨持續增加，顯示企業投資需求仍相當強勁，有助於支撐後續工業生產回升。從
企業預期來看，製造商預期9 月產量將成長 3.2%，顯示生產活動可望迅速回溫，
在供應鏈逐步恢復、庫存回補需求持續，以及穩健的企業資本支出支撐下，預計
第三季工業生產仍可維持正成長，且成長動能可望延續至年底。 
 8 月零售月減 1.2%，雖然表現略優於市場預期，但仍顯示民間消費動能正在
放緩。此次疲弱主要原因來自耐久財銷售下滑，其中家電銷售因空調等產品在換
機需求告一段落後明顯回落，汽車銷售也隨政府刺激措施效應逐漸消退而連續第
四個月下降。相較之下，食品、服裝等非耐久財銷售雖同步走弱，但降幅相對有
限 ， 反映薪資成長仍對消費提供部分支撐 。惟不利天候與通膨壓力仍是主要風險 ，
8 月至 9 月持續豪雨已影響服裝等非必需消費，而除米價以外的食品及商品價格
持續上漲，也正侵蝕家庭實質購買力，恐限制消費者信心與消費復甦力道。 
 9 月東京核心通膨年增率升至 2.7%，雖然東京都免費托兒及免費水費政策造
成的基期效應貢獻約 0.5 個百分點，但通膨升溫主因仍是價格上漲已由能源與食
品擴散至整體經濟。受石油價格上揚帶動，企業開始加速將原物料及薪資成本轉
嫁給消費者，帶動商品與服務價格普遍走高；租金通膨更升至 2%，創 1994 年以
來新高，公共交通及醫療等項目也開始調漲。隨著東京的物價壓力逐步反映至全
國數據，日本央行進一步升息的可能性升高。

四、中國—為刺激內需、引導產業走向，人行漸近式放寬政策工具 
9 月官方製造業 PMI 上升 0.3 點至 50.1，為近三個月高點，新訂單、出口訂
單指數分別為 50.5、50，並帶動生產指數上升至 51.7，不過，庫存指數下降、就
業指數加速衰退、企業展望減弱，皆拖累 PMI 指數水準。此外，投入、產出物價
指數分別上升至 60.8、54，顯示成本壓力加劇，但售價調整速度依然較為遲緩，
不利於獲利表現。官方非製造業 PMI 上升 1.2 點至 50.2，其中建築業活動指數大
增 3.4 點至 50.3，脫離連續八個月的衰退區間，主要是政府加大財政刺激力度，
推動重大建設開工；非製造業的從業人員指數為 45.9，顯示就業情況仍顯嚴峻，
亦較不利於內需表現。 
鑒於內需動能疲弱，人行於 9 月 29 日宣布下調抵押補充貸款(該貸款本質是
將資金引導至政府希望發展的領域)利率自 1.75%降至 1.50%，並擴大適用範圍，
將水網、新型電網、算力網、新一代通信網、城市地下管網及物流網等建設納入
支持，有助於提振內需動能。未來若內需復甦力道不如預期，不排除人行將進一
步降準或擴大結構性貨幣工具支持經濟；至於全面降息則仍須兼顧人民幣匯率及
中美利差等因素，可能性相對較低。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰國、菲律賓出口均達 2 成以上的高速成長

WinWay Technology
Valuation
We value WinWay using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum and likely expansion of its addressable
market. Our target price of NT$11,500 is derived from applying c.41x to 2H27E-1H28E EPS, higher than the upper end of its
long-term PE range, which we believe is justified given the company's entrenched position in the high-end AI GPU testing
supply chain, rising contribution from coaxial socket and SLT solutions, and the increasing value of test content per module.

With these structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play (rather than a
consumer electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant rating system rates the WinWay stock High Risk based on its historical volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price and our earnings forecasts include: 1) client and

sector concentration – specifically any delays in US AI GPU product roadmap could impact the business outlook for WinWay; 2)
intensifying competition in coaxial and hypersockets from global peers; 3) faster-than-expected testing algorithm
optimization, which could shorten testing durations and reduce the anticipated hardware demand multiplier; and 4) delay of
MEMS Phase 2 mass production.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day

For the month of October 2026, we estimate AirTAC’s monthly revenue will decrease 
to Rmb729mn (-10% MoM, +16% YoY)/NT$3,447mn (-10% MoM, +28% YoY) 
We expect October revenue will go down by 10% MoM to Rmb729mn/NT$3,447mn, 
mainly due to 19% fewer working days in Mainland China. However, we expect to see 
improvement in daily pneumatic revenue (Rmb32.3mn in Oct vs. Rmb 29.4mn in Sept) as 
the improvement in daily pneumatic revenue is driven by the strong growth from key 
customers, including electronics, followed by machine tools and battery, and we further 
expect demand to improve following the end of low season.

Earnings and TP revisions 
We revise down our 2026E earnings estimates by 1% to factor in actual 3Q26 revenue 
(revise down 2026 revenue by 1%). We revise down our 2027E/2028E earnings estimate 
by 1%/1% to factor in a more conservative assumption on battery and linear guide 
business (revise down AirTAC revenue by 1%/1% in 2027E/2028E). We continue to see 
the company focusing on cost control as well as experiencing a better product mix 
outlook, but we expect some offset by rising material cost (revise down 2026E/2028E 
OPM by 0.1/0.1ppt).

### Page 3

Maintain our Buy rating on AirTAC, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925), 
based on an unchanged target P/E of 26x on 2027 EPS.

Investment view: Accelerating monthly YoY from smaller segments could be a surprise. 
Maintain positive view on AirTAC 
We reiterate our Buy rating on AirTAC as we continue to believe the company is a key 
beneficiary of the factory automation/industrial automation localization trend in 
mainland China, and we expect the company will also benefit from the prolonged 
upcycle supported by the ongoing government stimulus policies. We see multiple key 
end-segments’ performance tracking ahead of the company’s 2026 full year guidance of 
20%+ YoY growth (see here), with battery/textile/machine tools/electronics delivering 
55%/46%/41%/23% YTD YoY growth while total revenue in Rmb is tracking at 23% YTD 
YoY growth. 
We continue to see solid monthly YoY growth from smaller segments, such as energy 
lighting and textile. However, the battery segment, which is the key growth driver in 
2025 (with 89% YoY in 2025), is seeing its first negative monthly YoY growth since Oct 
2024, while other major contribution sectors, such as electronics and automotive are 
seeing decelerating monthly YoY growth. As a result, we remain cautious on the 
potential of exaggerated orders (including advance or duplicate orders) despite new 
orders intake continuing to outpace shipments, and we will continue to monitor the 
underlying end-demand closely.

### Page 4

Maintain Buy on AirTAC with a lower 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925). 
Investment Thesis - AirTAC 
AirTAC is the 2nd-largest pneumatic supplier in mainland China with 28% market share 
in 2025, outpacing most local competitors, owing to its low-cost yet high quality 
product, and service quality reputation in most key cities in mainland China. We forecast 
its market share will reach 30% in 2026E and expect AirTAC to be the structural winner 
in a deflationary market, due to its: (1) strong market growth and leadership: AirTAC 
continues to expand its market share in mainland China driven by competitive pricing 
that is more than 10% lower vs. international peers (with 45%+ GM / 30-35% OPM) and 
high demand elasticity among mainland China customers; and (2) superior cost structure 
and financial health: with best-in-class cash cost and a solid balance sheet, featuring 
positive net cash over FCF since 2023/2024, we believe AirTAC is well-equipped to 
weather industry downturns and capitalize on market restructuring. 
Overall, we believe AirTAC will see better revenue/profitability in the long term given its 
share gainer position in the China pneumatic market and potential to gain share in the 
linear guide market (improved demand in the next 1-2 years). This should lead to 
strengthened valuation, vs. its recent trading range (trading below its 10-year avg. P/E 
multiple). We are Buy rated on AirTAC. 
Price target risks and methodology - AirTAC 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,895 is based on a target P/E of 26.0x on 
our 2027E EPS (0.25x STDV above avg of the last pneumatic upcycle). 
Key downside risks: (1) Stronger competition from peers; (2) sharper-than-expected 
Rmb depreciation, and (3) a longer-than-expected end-market demand downtrend in 
mainland China.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 12 Oct 2026 close.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
Overall September ODM NB shipments came in 2% below MSe: Quarter-end pull-
ins drove notebook shipments up 32% m/m, although volumes were 2% below MSe,
with weakness mainly coming from Wistron and Compal, and to a lesser extent,
Pegatron, reflecting slower NB shipments from Dell, as Dell accounted for ~60% of

Compal's NB shipment, and ~55% of Wistron's in CY25, on our estimates. Thus total
3Q NB builds came in at 25.2M units (-17% q/q, -26% y/y). This sequential decline
is sub-seasonal vs. the 15Y average seasonality of +5% q/q in 3Q.
Pull-forward demand appears to have come to an end, channel partners and
distributors have stocked up on inventory, and OEMs are slowing their sales into the
channel. We believe this is why our conversations with the ODMs suggest a slightly

weaker 4Q volume outlook than previously expected, despite favorable mix shift.
Thus, we lower our 4Q NB build estimate by 3% to 23.1M (-8% q/q, -29% y/y).
This sequential decline is sub-seasonal (despite an already lowered base in 3Q) vs.
the 15Y average seasonality of flat q/q in 4Q. We forecast October NB builds at
6.8M (-30% m/m, -33% y/y). Our revised forecasts imply CY26 builds of ~107.8M
units (-16% y/y) for the top five Taiwan NB ODMs.

Top five ODMs' September 2026 NB shipments were 9.7M units (+32% m/m, -
20% y/y), 2% below our estimate.
Total 3Q26 NB builds came in at 25.2M (-17% q/q, -26% y/y).
We see October 2026 NB builds at 6.8M units (-30% m/m, -33% y/y).

Our top five ODMs' CY26 NB build estimate is ~107.8M units (-16% y/y).

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商 
 雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商 
雙鴻成立於 1998 年 8 月 ， 為國內前二大NB 散熱模組廠 ， 過去主要生產散熱器 、
散熱片、散熱模組、散熱導管、風扇等，現在已轉型為提供客戶完整散熱解決
方案供應商。主要應用於筆記型電腦、桌上型電腦、智慧型手機及伺服器等領
域。2Q26 產品應用領域 Server 78%、PC 17%、VGA/Gaming 4%、及Others(手
機及車用) 1%。另外水冷業務比重於 1H26 約為 55~60%。

2Q26 EPS 10.41 元 
 2Q26 毛利率 28.4%，低於預期，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元 
雙鴻 2Q26 EPS 10.41 元，低於元富預估的 13.72 元，獲利達成率 76%。本業
來看，2Q26 營收 87 億元，+2%QOQ，+64%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 28.42%，較
1Q26 衰退 1.2 個百分點 ， 亦低於預估的30.5%， 主要是報廢品打消 ， 預期3Q26
毛利率回復上 1Q26 水準，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元。

雙鴻 3Q26 營收 97.91 億元，+12.5%QOQ，+64.3%YOY，營收達成率 94%，
低於預期，主要是七月營收有遞延認列，故推估基期過高，實際 3Q26 拉貨力
道未如預期顯著，預估 3Q26 毛利率 30%，預估 3Q26 EPS 14.13 元。

營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷兩塊
業務均樂觀

營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務均樂觀 
2025 年雙鴻水冷營收比重 35%，1Q26 提升至 55%，帶動公司業績大幅增長。
氣冷部分，2024 年月營收 2-2.5 億元，2025 年 4～4.5 億元，2026 年平均 6～
7 億元，氣冷水冷均樂觀，以 AWS TR3 來看，氣冷及水冷比重至少是 70~80%
對比 20~30%，TR4 將有機會為 100%全水冷 ， 水冷大趨勢不變 ，VR 9 月起量，
TR3 9 月起量 Compute tray 及 Switch tray 及 Rack manifolds 都有相關產品出
貨，Compute tray 水冷產值 3000usd 以上，Switch tray 水冷產值 1000usd 以
上，ASIC 水冷產值高於 GPU 客戶，2H26~2027 年 ASIC 增長可期。

1H26 GB 水冷營收中 Rack manifolds 佔比 30-35%、Cold Plate+inner manifolds
佔比 55-60%、CDU 5% ，2026 年可視為 CDU 元年，2027 年營收跳增可期。
公司展望 3Q26 營收+15~20%QOQ，實際+12.5%QOQ 低於預期，4Q26 營收
QOQ+雙位數，2026 年營收+70~85%YOY，3Q26 毛利率將回到 29.7%以上水
準，2027 年維持正向看法。

維持買進，目標價 1800 元

維持買進評等，目標價 1800 元 
2Q26 毛利率低於預期為一次性因素，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務樂觀，水冷擴張趨勢
明確，故維持買進，預估2026 年 EPS 55.74 元，預估 2027 年 EPS 90.36 元，
目標價 1800 元。(2027 PER 20x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

## Document 177: 7856漢測_20261008_UBS

Source PDF: all report pdf/7856漢測_20261008_UBS.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 27
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
Hermes Testing Solutions
Shifting-left, scaling-up: dual catalysts in chip
probing and CPO
Jack-of-all-trades for bespoke testing solutions in the semiconductor industry
We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 PT. HTSI is
uniquely positioned to benefit from two key semiconductor testing trends: shift-left
testing and the emerging CPO testing market. Leveraging its long-standing relationship

with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across electrical test engineering,
customised test equipment, thermal control modules, probe cards, and CPO testing
solutions. We forecast revenue growth of 127%/152%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, driven
by increasing adoption of thermal control modules for wafer-level test and initial
deployment of CPO insertion 2 and insertion 3 test equipment through ficonTEC.
Additional 2028E growth drivers include reflow tools, laser clean solutions, and MEMS

probe cards. The stock trades at 27/15x 2027/28E PE, versus ~35x NTM PE for handler-
equipment peers, offering re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified,
and its CPO presence expands. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Shift-left beneficiary with exceptional thermal control modules
Thermal control modules should be HTSI's largest growth driver over the next several
years. We expect increasing chip power consumption, rising package complexity, and

tighter probe pitches to drive adoption of advanced thermal control solutions used
alongside leading-edge probers, in additional to more wafer level insertions and longer
test time. We estimate HTSI will ship approximately 3,000 thermal-control modules in
2027E, up from around 1,200 units in 2026E, sales contribution rise from ~30% in
Q226 to >50% of sales by 2027E. The launch of its second-generation platform in
2027E, which carries 25-50% higher ASPs, should further improve profitability. We

forecast GM expansion from 55.5% in 2026E to 58.3%/60.0% in 2027E/28E.
Unique position to capture CPO upside
Through its partnership with German optical-automation leader ficonTEC, HTSI is
positioned to participate in the initial ramp of CPO testing equipment. HTSI supports
local manufacturing, installation, automation and after-sales service, making it a key
enabler of volume production. We estimate HTSI captures approximately 30% of
equipment value across the 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems we forecast for

delivery in 2027E, with further potential upside in 2028E as adoption expands.
Valuation: initiate coverage with a Buy rating and price target NT$8,880
We forecast EPS of NT$29.1 in 2026E (including a one-off employee share purchase
programme impact), rising to NT$153.3/286.5 in 2027E/28E. Our NT$8,880 price target
is based on 31x average 2028E PE, versus handler-equipment peers forward PE at
roughly 35x, reflecting HTSI's unique exposure to both shift-left testing, CPO, and our

estimate of 32% LT earnings CAGR. We initiate with a Buy rating.

Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will HTSI be able to keep its moat within chip probing thermal control modules?
Yes. We expect HTSI's competitive position to strengthen as chip power consumption, package
complexity, and probe density continue to increase. The company is developing next-generation
thermal control solutions, including liquid cooling technologies, while also expanding into specialized

chuck solutions designed to address wafer warpage. In addition, HTSI's close collaboration with
TSMC, TEL, Advantest, and other OSATs should reinforce its position within advanced wafer-level
testing.
Q: Will insertion 2 of CPO testing process be skipped?
No. We believe concerns surrounding insertion 2 adoption are overdone. While some industry
participants favor bypassing insertion 2 due to throughput and ROI considerations, doing so increases
the risk of defective optical engines being identified only after the components are delivered to

OSATs, creating yield accountability challenges and higher packaging losses. Based on our industry
studies, we expect insertion 2 testing to remain an important part of the CPO workflow over at least
the next two years as the industry focuses on improving PIC yields and overall testing efficiency.
We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 price target. HTSI
offers unique exposure to two of the semiconductor testing industry's most attractive growth drivers:

1) shift-left testing and 2) CPO testing. The industry's shift-left testing strategy will have more test
insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in addition to longer CP time. Leveraging its
longstanding relationship with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across thermal control
modules, electrical-test engineering services, customized equipment, and emerging CPO testing
solutions. We believe there could be re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified, and

its CPO presence expands.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI currently trades at just 15x of our 2028E EPS versus

handler-equipment peers forward PE at roughly 35x. We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO
testing equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on
our industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E,
this should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands. Our

reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR
vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for
re-rating.

Hermes Testing Solutions UBS Research
Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 3S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.

We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

Nonetheless, leaders within the testing sector will standout among competition, with
greater sales CAGR comparing to the industry.
We see HTSI's sales for the next few years to mainly be driven by 1) thermal control
module shipment and 2) CPO equipment shipment. In the past, thermal control

modules weren't necessary for chip probing, due to less TDP for the chips. However, as
new iterations of AI accelerators now got substantial power draw, this unlocks a brand
new TAM for HTSI to enter with their thermal control modules to pair with probers, in
order to prevent probe card damages.
 Robust thermal control module shipments.  We estimate HTSI to ship
3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2027E/28E, with continuous demand in
future years, bringing their 2026-31E semiconductor equipment sales CAGR to

48%.

 Thermal control module ASP expansion.  Gen 2 thermal control modules will
start to ship in 2027E which will drive tool ASP up around 25-50%.
 Demand supported by Advantest expansions. We believe the 3,000 thermal
control modules shipment forecast for 2027E is not far-fetched, as we read-across
from Advantest's announced capacity expansion plan (testers will be paired with
probers with a 1:1 ratio) with increasing capacity up to 5,000 testers within

FY2027/3 and they also announced a 10,000-unit target within CY2028.
Although the testers capacity expansion plan includes testers for final test, we do
see a good portion to be allocated to chip probing given shift-left testing trend
increasing both testing insertions and testing time before chip packaging.
 CPO is another brand new TAM for semiconductor testing.  As insertion 2
and 3 testing equipment demand starts to rise in 2027 with scale-out CPO

adoption, we see HTSI to ship 40/70 insertion 2 and 3 equipment in 2027E, with
this number growing in 2028E to 80/160 and higher in future years as CPO
proliferates, and driving 52% 2026-31E test equipment & engineering services
sales CAGR.
 CPO to see content increase. Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment may start to ship in
2027E, with higher throughput and tool ASP .
 Insertion 2 might scale down, but not happening anytime soon. Insertion 2
equipment shipment might come down in 2029E and beyond, as foundries scale-

up yield for photonic IC (PIC) in order to lessen the time consuming insertion 2
testing burden.

Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe HTSI is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers and
capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see HTSI is uniquely positioned as
the main beneficiary of two of the strongest growth drivers within semiconductor
testing: testing shift-left and CPO.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing

### Page 5

The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging.
Figure 4: CP is set to grow substantially with shift-left, each packaging step set to be MORE costly than the prior step

RDL / RWAT testing Wafer-level CP (WLCP)
CoWChiplet (if use SoIC) CoW
CP (Die A)
CP (Die B)
Assembly Test (AST)
oS
Source: UBS
The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while
defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed

testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million
(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall

integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of
wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level

chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

Hermes Testing (HTSI) is a key beneficiary of this trend. Leveraging its long-standing
relationship with TSMC and expertise in semiconductor test solutions, HTSI supplies

advanced thermal control modules used alongside leading-edge probers (made by
Tokyo Electron), particularly for AI applications. These thermal control modules are
required for high TDP semiconductor wafer probe in order to maintain the temperature

### Page 6

of the chuck to avoid needle burns or probe card damages. We estimate HTSI will ship
approximately 3,000 thermal-management modules in 2027E, up from 1,200 in 2026E,
supporting sales growth of 127%/152% in 2026E/27E. HTSI is also the partner for
prober installation, prober maintenance and services. In addition, HTSI provides high-
end chuck processing services, an area that should benefit from increasing package sizes
and higher wafer warpage associated with advanced packaging.

Beyond thermal control modules, HTSI also supplies consumables and customised
solutions for wafer-level testing, including probe card cleaning sheets and laser-based
probe head cleaning systems. As probing intensity increases under the shift-left testing
model, demand for cleaning and maintenance solutions should rise alongside probe
card utilisation. Currently, cleaning sheets is around 5% of sales, but we believe it could
gain traction in 2027/28E and beyond. We see additional upside from future modular

laser cleaning systems that could be integrated directly with probers, further expanding
HTSI's content opportunity within advanced wafer-level testing.
CPO testing fuelling future growth for semiconductor testing
As interconnect bottlenecks increasingly shift from the memory wall to the copper wall,
we believe co-packaged optics (CPO) adoption is inevitable over the longer term. Testing
remains one of the key bottlenecks to CPO deployment, as optical validation differs

fundamentally from the electrical testing processes that have dominated semiconductor
manufacturing for decades. As a result, CPO is creating a new testing opportunity across
the semiconductor equipment and consumables ecosystem. We have written
extensively on CPO and opportunities that it brings to the semiconductor supply chain,
see our CPO deep dive , our thoughts on CPO timing , 2026 SiPhIA conference
takeaways, and SEMICON 2026 takeaways.
Various test equipment suppliers are scrambling to come up with ideal testing

equipment across the four insertions of CPO testing, with solutions to ensure both
optical and electrical signal integrity of the CPO solutions. The four insertions are:
 Insertion 1: Electronic IC (EIC)/Photonic IC (PIC) test.  Single-side wafer test
that has dedicated probe cards to test the fabricated EIC/PIC wafers.
 Insertion 2: Optical/Electrical test. Double sided wafer test, testing the hybrid
bonded (e.g. via TSMC's COUPE) EIC and PIC wafers by an opto-electrical test that

verifies the electrical signals on the top and optical signal on the bottom of the
hybrid bonded wafers.
 Insertion 3: Optical engine (OE) test.  The OE tests are done to ensure the
quality of the OEs and with FAU (Fiber Array Unit) attached, to ensure the signal
integrity of the OEs. The testing equipment needs to be able to handle 224G per
channel data rates, requiring high unit per hour (UPH) throughput, meticulous dirt
mitigation, and maintaining a near 100% contact rating on high-speed sockets.

 Insertion 4: ASIC final test.  This final test step is established post the OEs are
packaged together with the ASIC. The complete ASIC package (now with CPO) is
now tested for opto-electrical functionalities.
As it stands, insertion 2 and insertion 3 are the two areas where there are the most
debates in the industry. Insertion 3 suppliers such as Chroma has noted the trend of
“shift-to-middle” paradigm centered on insertion 3. In high-density scale-out switches

deploying up to 32 optical engines (e.g. Nvidia’s Spectrum-X architectures), any OE
defect results in package scrap at the CoWoS level, making 100% known good die
(KGD) validation at the singulated OE stage an absolute commercial requirement.

With its 25 years of precision test equipment expertise, ficonTEC is an early mover for
CPO test equipment and has key know-how for optical alignment, which is
quintessential for optical test. HTSI, through its partnership with ficonTEC, is set to be a
major beneficiary once CPO proliferates in 2027E-28E. As the partner of ficonTEC,
Hermes Testing is the key enabler that can help ficonTEC achieve HVM for its CPO
testing equipment. As of now, our view is that both insertion 2 and insertion 3 should be

required within the next two years, despite the difficulties surrounding double-sided
wafer probing (insertion 2), as the yield for OEs are still not high enough to avoid
defective dies. Finding out known-good OEs will be crucial before the FAU coupling
process during the latter stages of CPO packaging, to prevent impeding the final
package with multiple OEs attached to the switch ASIC (e.g. 32 OEs on Nvidia's
Spectrum-X). We estimate Hermes Testing will ship 40/70 units of insertion 2/3 testing

equipment in 2027E, with potential upside to the numbers as we view Hermes Testing
will likely assist with the ramp up for Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment in 2027E. Gen 2
insertion equipment will come with better throughput and ASP .

Beyond insertions 2 and 3, HTSI is also positioned to participate in insertion 1 testing
through customised prober solutions for photonic integrated circuit (PIC) testing. We
believe these solutions could eventually be packaged as licensable intellectual property,

creating an additional long-term growth opportunity as CPO adoption broadens. While
near-term testing growth is driven by AI accelerators, CPO represents the next major
expansion cycle for semiconductor testing. We believe HTSI is uniquely positioned to
participate across multiple test insertions, providing exposure to a potentially significant
source of incremental revenue beginning in 2027E.

HTSI's unique position for new TAM of thermal control
modules and CPO test equipment
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan, as a spin-off
from Hermes-Epitek. The company provides semiconductor testing and inspection

solutions, engineering services, customized equipment, and test consumables. HTSI
maintains a global service footprint across Taiwan (Hsinchu, Tainan), Mainland China
(Suzhou), Japan (Tokyo, Kumamoto), Singapore, Malaysia, and the United States
(Arizona), enabling close support for leading semiconductor customers.
Its main business segments are structured around three pillars:
 Semiconductor equipment and customized products : HTSI utilises a dual-

track strategy of independent R&D and also equipment agency sales. They offers

### Page 9

highly customized solutions designed to address specific client pain points and
bottlenecks. Key products include: thermal control modules that pair with a 1:1
ratio with wafer-level test probers, electron beam inspection equipment, white
light interference optical inspection systems, laser probe cleaning machines, and
the SYMPHONY fluxless reflow system.
 Testing equipment engineering services:  The company operates the largest
engineering service team in Taiwan, consisting of over 340 technical professionals.

The engineering service team provides extensive equipment life-cycle support,
including installation, relocations, modification, testing, routine maintenance, and
repair services. CPO related equipment sales/services will be recognized within this
segment, as CPO are not direct HTSI products, but rather sales recognition via its
partnership with ficonTEC.
 Probe card design, manufacturing, and cleaning materials:  HTSI delivers a
comprehensive product portfolio that supports the entire probe card life-cycle. For

probe cards, they supply membrane, cantilever, and vertical probe cards. As they
are small in scale, HTSI targets niche probe card applications such as RF, 5G/6G,
millimeter wave, LCD driver, low end logics, TIA (Transimpedance Amplifier), etc. In
addition to the three existing probe technologies, HTSI also collaborates with MJC
in Japan for MEMS probe development, targeting sales in 2028E for high-end, AI/
HPC related applications. Cleaning materials is another important business within

this segment, as HTSI source and process sand paper or other materials to offer
cleaning sheets to clean the probe cards, removing oxidations, unwanted particles
and substances that accumulated on the probe cards during the testing process.
The sales resiliency in the coming years mainly stems from its robust relationship with key
industry players, especially as an engineering and service provider with TSMC for testing
solutions. This sets up a solid foundation for its customized equipment business, as they

know the specific testing bottleneck to tackle. For instance, as Hermes-Epitek
manufacturers the probers for Tokyo Electron (TEL) and HTSI helps with installation,
services, and maintenance of the probers, they understand the thermal bottlenecks that
the wafer-level test industry has been facing and can effectively come up with module
solutions to solve this.
Margins set to expand with value-added products and services
The company has demonstrated strong execution in advanced wafer-level testing, with

H126 revenue of NT$2.2bn already exceeding 90% of FY25 revenue (NT$2.4bn).
Profitability has also improved significantly, with GM increasing from 42.1% in 2025 to
54.9% in H126.
With share of value-added products and services set to rise in 2027E in sales mix, HTSI is
set to see GM and operating leverage expansion. As a niche engineering service provider
that has robust ties to leading foundries and OSATs, we believe HTSI has substantial
pricing power and estimate HTSI' GM for thermal control modules to be around 65%,

with GM for CPO equipment of around 60% as HTSI provides an engineering-service-
like business for it. We believe these factors will help the company to reach a 60% long-
term GM range.
OPM is also set to expand with customized equipment sales rise in total sales mix. If we
exclude the one-time expense to be recognized during Q326 for employee stock
benefits (we estimate around NT$1bn), YoY opex growth will be around 99% for
2027E, with downside risk if the company conducts tight cost control measures. We

forecast the company's OPM will expand from 19.5% in 2026E, to 44.3%/47.0% in
2027/28E.

Source: LSEG, UBS
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI still trades at just 15x 2028E P/E. We
believe market expectations for 2027E CPO testing equipment shipments remain
conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our industry analysis, we
estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this should
be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3
shipment due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO

adoption expands. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM
level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for re-rating.
We believe the market is not fully reflecting three key growth drivers:
1) Shift-left testing is still underappreciated.
As advanced packaging costs rise and AI accelerators become increasingly complex,
more thorough testing is done at the wafer-level. This should drive sustained demand

for HTSI's thermal control modules, probe card consumables, and other wafer-level
testing solutions.
2) CPO revenue potential remains underestimated.
Investor expectations for 2027 CPO equipment shipments, particularly insertion 2
testing systems, remain conservative. Based on our industry studies, we forecast HTSI
will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, with further potential
upside in 2028E as CPO adoption broadens.
3) New products provide additional optionality.

Beyond its core businesses, HTSI is expanding into higher-value solutions including mass-
reflow tools, MEMS probe cards and laser cleaning systems, creating incremental
growth opportunities that we believe are largely absent from current expectations.
We value HTSI using 31x average 2028E PE, underpinned by a 32% 2027-31E earnings
CAGR. We use 2028E PE as our view is that 2028 is the year for thermal control modules
and CPO adoption grow from the initial ramping up stage to mass adoption stage. We

believe using handlers as a peer group is justifiable for a unique company like HTSI, with
thermal control modules for chip probing sharing similar characteristics as active thermal
control used for final test, in addition to Hon Precision and Chroma having exposure to
CPO test equipment. For equipment vendors, the peer group's average NTM PE post
2022 is around 35x, and the group is currently trading at around 41x NTM PE (see
What's Priced In chart).

HTSI is trading at NT$4,190 as of 6
Oct 2026

Source: UBS estimates
Risk to the current share price is heavily skewed (2.2:1) to the upside.
UPSIDE (NT$10,000): Our upside scenario reflects stronger than expected thermal
module and CPO equipment shipment in 2027-28E, with additional upside from better
traction of probe cards and mass reflow tools, forming 157%/91% YoY sales growth in
2027/28E and even higher operating leverage with OPM of 46.3%/50.0% in 2027/28E.

This scenario implies a 34x 2028E PE, a upside driving by more equipment demand
stemming for robust shift-left test induced equipment bottleneck, alongside CPO
proliferation with scale-up CPO equipment pull-in in 2028E.
BASE (NT$8,880): Our price target of NT$8,880 is based on 31x 2028E PE. We expect an
earnings CAGR of 32% in 2027-31E. With shift-left trend for more thermal module
requirement during testing, alongside 40/70 CPO insertion 2/3 shipment. We see these

growth drivers driving YoY sales growth from a high base of 127% in 2026E to
152%/78% YoY in 2027/2028E, with OPM of 44.3%/47.0% in 2027/28E.
DOWNSIDE (NT$1,500): Our downside scenario reflects lower shipment of thermal
control modules due to less advanced prober demand with less insertion 2 and 3
equipment shipment due to bottlenecks with CPO production, alongside limited
additional growth drivers to be picked up in 2028 such as MEMS probe card and mass

reflow tools. This leads to weaker YoY sales growth of 67%/50% in 2027/28E and
operating margin of 29.3%/32.0% in 2027/28E as equipment shipment fails to scale.
This scenario implies a 14x 2028E PE, as valuation de-rate with dimmed outlook without
CPO and limited upside from thermal control module shipments.

Company Description
Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu,
Taiwan. It focuses on providing semiconductor testing and
inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical support,
and maintenance services. Its main business segments are
customized semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and
probe cards and other materials.
Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC

applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It focuses on
providing semiconductor testing and inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical
support, and maintenance services. Its main business segments are customized
semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and probe cards and other materials.

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Hermes Testing based on PE methodology.
Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less
investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development

such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected front-end/CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in
Hermes Testing gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.
- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from

either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
Hermes Testing losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
Hermes Testing, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.
- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of

the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact Hermes
Testing’s margins for equipment shipments.

of a number of short term factors in a product known as the 'Quantitative Research Review'.  The views for this month can be found below.
Views contained in this assessment on a particular stock reflect only the views on those short term factors which are a different timeframe to

the 12-month timeframe reflected in any equity rating set out in this note.  For previous responses please make reference to (i) previous UBS
Global Research reports; and (ii) where no applicable research report was published that month, the Quantitative Research Review which can
be found at https://neo.ubs.com/quantitative, or contact your UBS sales representative for access to the report or the Quantitative Research
Team on ubs-quant-answers@ubs.com. A consolidated report which contains all responses is also available and again you should contact your

UBS sales representative for details and pricing or the Quantitative Research Team on the email above.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

## Document 208: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

## Document 235: AI数据中心泡沫将近？英伟达支持的Nscale估值接近腰斩，微软也撤了

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI数据中心泡沫将近？英伟达支持的Nscale估值接近腰斩，微软也撤了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

英国新云计算公司 Nscale 因电⼒接⼊延误遭微软撤单，且 IPO 估值被投资者要求降⾄ 160 亿
⾄ 190 亿美元，较原计划缩⽔超四成。这成为⼀个⽉内第五宗折戟的 AI 数据中⼼上市案，反
应出供电瓶颈、巨额亏损与⾼融资成本正重创 AI 基础设施泡沫。
摘要：
英伟达背书、坐拥千亿美元合同承诺的英国新云计算公司 Nscale ，正遭遇与⼀个⽉前澳⼤利亚
同⾏ Firmus 如出⼀辙的困境： IPO 估值被投资者要求接近腰斩，微软宣告撤出关键数据中⼼项
⽬。
据彭博报道， Nscale 此前寻求以约 300 亿美元估值上市，但已有⼀批投资者明确表⽰， 160 亿⾄
190 亿美元 " 更可能获得买家认可 "—— 较原定估值缩⽔逾四成。与此同时，据路透援引《电讯
报》的报道，微软已终⽌与 Nscale 位于英国埃塞克斯郡洛顿（ Loughton ）数据中⼼园区的合作
协议，原因是英国国家电⽹官员告知 Nscale ，该站点最早可能要到 2030 年代才能获得供电。微
软对此拒绝置评。

这是不到⼀个⽉内第五宗 AI 数据中⼼上市计划遭遇挫折的案例，前四宗分别涉及软银旗下 SB
Energy 、 Firmus 、菲律宾 PLDT ⼦公司 Vitro 及⿊⽯旗下 AirTrunk 。⾼盛信贷团队将这⼀系列事件
归结为 "AI 交易遭遇资⾦成本 " ，⽽信贷市场早在数周前已通过债券价格和信⽤违约互换的恶化率
先发出预警。
微软撤局：供电⽆期，锚定客⼾先⾏离场
洛顿项⽬的失败，反映出当前 AI 基础设施建设的核⼼⽭盾：合同可以⼀天之内签订，电⽹连接却
可能遥遥⽆期。
据路透报道，微软终⽌协议的直接导⽕索是国家电⽹的告知 —— 该站点直⾄ 2030 年代都⽆望接
⼊电⼒供应。 Nscale ⽅⾯回应称，与微软的 " 关系依然稳固且⻓期 " ，将此次问题定性为 " 伦敦范
围内的电⽹问题 " ，并表⽰正在 " 探索替代能源⽅案 " 。然⽽，锚定客⼾已然离场，这⼀表态难以
弥补实质性的商业损失。
微软今年已不⽌⼀次选择撤出第三⽅ AI 算⼒项⽬ —— 据彭博此前报道，今年 6 ⽉，微软曾拒绝了

⼀笔价值 30 亿美元的甲⻣⽂云计算合同。当全球现⾦储备最为充裕的超⼤规模云服务商开始对
租⽤算⼒挑剔起来，主打 " ⽐任何⼈更快上线算⼒ " 的新云计算公司的核⼼商业逻辑便受到直接挑
战。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

挪威旗舰园区的情况同样不容乐观。 Nscale 位于北极圈以北的格洛姆峡湾（ Glomfjord ）设施已
延误六个⽉，原因涵盖冷却系统泄漏、当地法规、建设延误及⽕灾事故。计划中 30 兆⽡的容
量，⽬前实际运⾏的仅有 6.4 兆⽡。
千亿合同下的真实账本： 4% 的落地率与 107 倍的估值泡沫
Nscale 的招股说明书在头部数字上颇为亮眼，但细究之下，数据显⽰出现实与承诺之间的巨⼤
鸿沟。
公司披露的合同总价值⾼达 1034 亿美元，较两年半前创⽴时的约 1 亿美元急剧扩张。然⽽实际在
运营的合同价值仅为 26 亿美元；已上线容量为 55 兆⽡（其中 48 兆⽡为租⽤托管），⽽已签署的 IT
容量承诺约为 1346 兆⽡，实际落地⽐例仅约 4% 。微软（合同上限 438 亿美元）与 Anthropic （合
同上限 446 亿美元，均来⾃尚未获得融资的西弗吉尼亚园区）合计占订单储备的约 85% ，⽽
Anthropic 合同条款允许其⽆附加义务退出，且相关融资尚未到位。
财务层⾯， Nscale 2026 年上半年营收为 1.406 亿美元，净亏损达 10.2 亿美元，即每获得 1 美元收

⼊，便亏损约 7 美元。按上半年营收年化计算，约 2.8 亿美元的年营收对应 300 亿美元估值，意味
着市销率⾼达约 107 倍。即便按投资者认可的 160 亿⾄ 190 亿美元区间定价，市销率仍在 57 ⾄ 68
倍之间。
此外，英国《⾦融时报》曾披露 Nscale 在招股材料中淡化了其与字节跳动的关系 —— 后者去年
贡献了其近四分之三的销售额。 Nscale 于 9 ⽉ 18 ⽇公开提交招股⽂件，强制等待期早已届满，但
据彭博报道，公司⽬前仍在 " 处理美国证券交易委员会的意⻅ " ，尚未启动正式路演。
连环折戟：新云计算 IPO 窗⼝接连关闭
Nscale 的困境并⾮孤例，⽽是近⼀个⽉内 AI 数据中⼼融资浪潮退潮的缩影。
这轮折戟浪潮始于 9 ⽉ 22 ⽇：软银旗下 SB Energy 原计划为全球最⼤数据中⼼募资，因投资者对
其逾 500 亿美元估值望⽽却步， IPO 被迫延期⾄今仍未启动正式营销。随后是 Firmus 先削减 IPO
价格 25% ，继⽽彻底撤回上市计划；菲律宾 PLDT ⼦公司 Vitro 将数据中⼼ REIT 上市推迟⾄ 2027

年；⿊⽯旗下 AirTrunk 即便将收益率提升⾄约 6% ，其新加坡 REIT 的 15 亿美元 IPO 同样延⾄ 2027
年。
信贷市场早已率先发出警报。， CoreWeave 的信⽤违约互换价差已飙升⾄历史纪录，隐含五年内
违约概率约为 63% ；其 2032 年⽆担保债券收益率触及 15%。 Gartner 预测，逾 80% 的新云计算公
司将在 2030 年前终⽌独⽴运营。⾼盛信贷团队在其周末报告中写道：
" 资本并未消失，只是变得更具甄别⼒ …… 市场⽇益区分那些能够⾃主融资增⻓的公司，与那些
增⻓依赖融资持续可得的公司。 "

### Page 3

⾼盛⾼产值信贷团队还指出，样本⾼收益数据中⼼项⽬融资债券的平均价格已从 6 ⽉的 101.5 美
元跌⾄ 10 ⽉的 94.7 美元， " 建设⻛险、再融资敞⼝、租⼾担保、电⼒供应及审批许可 " 正成为信贷
承销的核⼼考量 —— ⽽恰恰是电⼒供应与审批问题，直接导致了 Nscale 在洛顿失去微软这⼀锚
定客⼾。
估值重定价蔓延股市：科技板块连遭抛售
信贷市场的压⼒已充分传导⾄权益资产。 CoreWeave 上周市值蒸发约四分之⼀， Nebius 两周内
累计下跌 32% 。据⾼盛全球股票衍⽣品团队报告，超⼤规模云服务商的信⽤利差已扩张⾄ 2025
年以来最宽⽔平，⽽历史上当利差处于类似区间时，相关股票的价格⽔平较当前低约 15% 。
⾼盛技术、媒体与电信专家 Peter Callahan 指出，纳斯达克 100 指数上周录得 2025 年 4 ⽉以来最
差单周表现，跌幅约 550 个基点，半导体板块领跌约 11.5% 。费城半导体指数（ SOX ）已连续⼋
个交易⽇下跌，创 2008 年以来最⻓连跌纪录；半导体板块估值已从今年夏季约 29 倍远期市盈率

压缩⾄约 17 倍。⾼盛 AI 受益股票篓⼦较 6 ⽉⾼点相对⼤盘已回落 25% 。
下⼀步：私募市场或是唯⼀出路
就眼下⽽⾔， Nscale ⾯临两条路径。据彭博报道，相关讨论仍在进⾏， IPO 计划的细节可能发⽣
变化，尚⽆最终决定。
选项之⼀是接受估值打折，按 160 亿⾄ 190 亿美元定价上市—— 这仅略⾼于今年 3 ⽉ Series C 融资
时 146 亿美元的估值，对于今年 9 ⽉由 Dan Loeb 旗下 Third Point 领衔、英伟达、 Apollo 及 Citadel
跟投、合计注⼊ 33.6 亿美元的 Pre-IPO 投资⼈⽽⾔，这将是⼀个远⽐当初预期更为黯淡的结果。
选项之⼆是效仿 Firmus ，转向私募市场—— 但即便是 Blue Owl 也未能为⼀个 40 亿美元的数据中
⼼项⽬找到第三⽅资⾦。
更⼤的市场背景同样不容忽视。银⾏家⾯临 " 多重⻛险交织 " 的局⾯， IPO 市场正对除 Anthropic 以
外的⼏乎所有 AI 项⽬趋于冻结。⽽ Anthropic 作为 Nscale 订单储备的两⼤⽀柱之⼀，其⾃⾝上市

计划亦可能⾯临延迟。届时， Nscale 将与最⼤客⼾之⼀争夺同⼀批 AI 主题投资者的资⾦ —— 这
⼀处境颇为微妙。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

## Document 297: EcoNews of 5 October 2026 (2026-10-05)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 5 October 2026 (2026-10-05).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.36 / 5 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION / EUROZONE
Resilient corporate sentiment, upside inflation surprises. The Euro -
pean Commission’s Economic Sentiment Index for September showed
continued recovery in industry (+1.2 pts to -3.8, a 3.5 year high), with
a clear rebound in production and employment expectations, while

services improved further (+0.5 pt to 6.1). By contrast, household
confidence and sentiment in construction weakened further, as did
the composite index. These surveys broadly corroborate the PMIs,
with the September manufacturing PMI revised up by 0.2 pt to 52.9.
The unemployment rate held steady at a low 6.4%. Meanwhile, the
September flash inflation estimate came in above consensus at 3.8%.
The increase was driven mainly by energy (18.8% y/y, +4.5 pp) and

services (3.2%, +0.2 pp). Non-energy industrial goods inflation eased
slightly to 1.1% y/y, but the trend is firming: the 3m/3m annualised
rate is at a three-year high of 2.5%. Speaking before the data release,
ECB President Lagarde argued for a “measured response” and noted
that the increase in bond yields was delivering some of the needed
restraint. Tightening is also occurring via the cost-of-borrowing for
mortgage loans, which increased in August for the fifth consecutive

month to 3.6% (+6 bp m/m), a level unseen since September 2024.
It was unchanged for loans to non-financial corporations (3.8%).
Upcoming: ECB minutes (Thursday) and EU Trade Commissioner
Šefcovic's visit to Beijing (Thursday-Friday)
France. In the eye of the bond market storm. The government’s draft
budget for 2027, despite targeting significant adjustment (see our
detailed analysis), failed to stop the surge in the OAT yield, which
passed 4.9% on Friday morning (a high unseen since 2002), while the

OAT-bund spread reached a peak of 151 bps before easing somewhat
along with the global bond market selloff. (The peak coincided with
a statement by Bundesbank President Nagel that the ECB’s instru -
ments are focused on monetary policy transmission, not any spread
level). As expected, the draft budget law targets a deficit of 5% of GDP
(down from 5.4% in 2026), implying EUR 54 bn of savings, of which
about 2/3 coming from reduced spending. France’s independent fiscal

watchdog commended the spending moderation effort but pointed out
optimistic economic assumptions.
Meanwhile, inflation surprised on the upside: HICP inflation accele -
rated to 3.4% y/y in September (consensus: 3.1%), from 2.6% in August.
The sharp rise in energy prices (21.2%) was amplified by a 10% y/y
increase in the price of fresh food (linked to the summer drought).
Core inflation rose to 1.6% y/y in September, up from 1.3% in August
(services inflation at 2.2%; +0.3 pp m/m). The rise in industrial pro -

ducer prices accelerated to 6% y/y in August from 4.3% in July (+67%
versus +50% for refined products). Higher fuel prices also weighed on
household consumption, which fell by -0.5% m/m in August. On the
brighter side, the September manufacturing PMI was revised up by
0.3 points to 50.6.
Germany. Inflation accelerates to 3-year high. HICP inflation reached
3.3% y/y in September (vs. 2.9% in August and 3.2% expected) ac -
cording to the flash estimate (the highest since December 2023),

driven by energy prices. Core inflation rose by 2.4% (same as in Au -
gust) and services inflation edged down to 2.7% (vs. 2.8% in August).
The unemployment rate was stable in September at 6.4%.
Upcoming: August factory orders (Tuesday) and industrial production
(Wednesday)
Italy and Spain. Upside inflation surprises, business sentiment gains.
In Italy, HICP rose by 4.1% (vs. 3.2% in August), while core rose by 1.7%
(vs. 1.5% in August). In Spain, HICP inflation accelerated to 5% y/y

(vs. 4.6% in August), while core inflation reached 3.1% y/y (vs. 2.9%
earlier). Business sentiment, measured by the European Commission’s
ESI, rose slightly in both manufacturing and services in Italy, and
more significantly in Spain, where unlike in the rest of Europe, consu -
mer sentiment improved as well. Less positively, Italy’s unemploy -
ment rate unexpectedly rose to 6.2%.
UNITED KINGDOM
Bold new policy paths and upward growth revision. Prime Minister

Burnham broke several policy taboos in his Labour Party Conference
Speech, notably reconsidering Brexit and abandoning the so-called
“triple lock” guaranteeing that state pensions rise each year by the
highest of inflation, wages, or 2.5%. These proposals would be taken
up in the next Parliament. He also vowed to dismantle the legacy
of Thatcherism in public services and utilities. Real GDP growth for
Q2 2026 was revised upward to 0.5% q/q (vs. 0.4% previously, fol -

lowing 0.6% in Q1), driven by a stronger-than-expected performance
in trade and investment. Inflation expectations in the Bank of En -
gland’s Decision Maker Panel rose to 3.9% for own prices and 3.3%
for CPI one year ahead.
UNITED STATES
Solid growth, underwhelming payrolls. Q2 GDP growth was revised
up to 2.2% annualised (from 1.5%), with broad-based revisions to in -
vestment and consumption. The September ISM manufacturing in -
dex edged down (-0.1 pt to 54.5) on a drop in production. However,

new orders, a leading indicator, improved, and the employment in -
dex held at its highest level in four years. Payrolls rose by 29,000
in September, well below expectations, while the two months prior
were revised down by a combined 60,000. Job gains in healthcare
(+23k) and leisure/hospitality (+10k) were partly offset by losses in
Government (-10k). The unemployment rate rose to 4.2% (+0.1pp)
on higher labour force participation. Private hourly earnings growth

continued to slow (3% y/y, lowest since mid-2021). The August per -
sonal consumption expenditure (PCE) inflation report surprised to
the downside (3.4% vs. 3.7% expected), and core PCE was unchanged
at 3%. On the household side, the saving rate fell sharply (4.1% of
disposable income in August, from 4.6% in July), explaining the unex -
pected resilience of consumption (+0.6% in August inflation-adjusted).
All in all, this suggests the Fed will continue to raise interest rates

but doesn’t need to rush. We expect the next 25 bps hike in December.
Upcoming: September ISM services (Monday); FOMC Minutes (We -
dnesday).
JAPAN
Booming business sentiment. The Tankan survey index of large ma -
nufacturers rose to +24 (+2 pts q/q), while small and medium en -
terprises saw significant gains (+14; +5 pts q/q and +23; +5 pts q/q
respectively), driven by demand for semiconductors and production
equipment. Services sentiment weakened slightly (+35; -2 pts q/q for

large firms). Investment intentions remain strong among large cor -
porations and are showing signs of recovery in SMEs.

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.36 / 5 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
ECONEWS Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Manufacturing PMIs generally stronger. The index rose to 56.7 in
Taiwan (the highest level since 2021) and 53.9 in South Korea, boosted
by strong global demand for AI goods. Korean exports increased by
a record 83.5% y/y in September (of which chips: +263% y/y), mostly
led by export prices. The manufacturing PMI also improved and was

comfortably above 50 in India, Indonesia and Thailand. It declined
slightly in Vietnam (to 51.9). Malaysia and the Philippines stood out
with PMIs falling slightly below 50. PMIs also generally improved
in central Europe. In Latin America, PMIs were more mixed: falling
sharply in Brazil to 44.8, its lowest level since early 2023, but slightly
improving in Mexico (to 50) and Colombia (to 54.6).
ASIA
China. Better outlook on small stimulus and higher PMIs. Following

a string of data showing too sluggish domestic demand to meet the
Government’s growth target of 4.5 to 5%, the State Council announced
a package of stimulus measures, including the expansion of some re -
lending facilities aimed at financing tech/innovation, agriculture and
SMEs; new spending to be financed by carried-over local govern -
ment debt quotas; and a small interest rate subsidy on mortgages.
The latest PMIs published by the NBS and by RatingDog improved

marginally, with both now showing expansion in both manufacturing
and services.
EUROPE, MIDDLE EAST
Poland. Strong uptick in September’s inflation reading. Inflation
came in at 4.0% y/y in September (the highest level since June
2025) after 3.4% y/y in August, driven by higher prices at the pump.
The increase led the government to lower its VAT rate on fuel to 8%
(23% previously) from October until year-end, which should help ease
inflationary pressures. Overall, inflation may not significantly exceed

the Central bank’s target on average in 2026. The Central Bank may
nonetheless consider one rate hike by year end.
Türkiye. A financial scandal with limited macro impact. A Ponzi sche-
me collapse has impacted around 455k retail investors with assets
reportedly worth about 1% of GDP . The crisis was swiftly handled
by the authorities to prevent financial contagion. 131 funds and two
home-finance institutions involved in the scandal were suspended

and taken over by public banks for orderly and fair disposal of their
assets. Three investment banks and 2 factoring companies were
transferred to the Savings Deposit Insurance Fund. So far, 217 people
have been subjected to legal action, of whom 90 have been jailed
pending trial. Since September 9, the BIST-30 index lost 10% but is
still 25% higher than at end-2025, and foreign investors bought equi -
ties at the highest pace in 3 months last week, suggesting a buy-the-

dip reading of the situation. There has been no or marginal spillover
effects on the exchange rate, domestic interest rates and sovereign
spreads. At this stage, contagion risk to the banking sector is very low
but the damage to small investors may have negative spillovers on
consumption through negative wealth effect. Separately, the manu -
facturing PMI fell further in September to 47.9, signaling deepening
contraction.
Saudi Arabia. Budget deficit jumps on cost of war. Budget deficit is

now forecasted at USD 65.4 bn this year (-4.9% of GDP), up from
USD 44.3 bn (-3.3% of GDP) in the initial financing bill. Revenue is
expected to be slightly higher (+3.7%) as high oil prices more than
offset the decline in oil export volume. But spending has been revised
up by 9.3%. The new growth projection is also bearish, with GDP
expected to contract by -3.6% due to the decline in oil activity while
consensus sees economy growing by +0.2%.
LATIN AMERICA

Brazil. A tight runoff, a tough policy mix inheritance. Senator Flávio
Bolsonaro and Brazil’s incumbent President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva
will face off in a runoff on October 25 th after neither won a majority
in Sunday’s general election (47% vs. 45% of the vote respectively).
Since Brazil returned to democracy in 1989, no candidate who trailed
in the first round has won the runoff. The next President will inherit
a slowing economy and a difficult policy mix: pressing ahead with

fiscal consolidation while normalising monetary policy amid recur -
ring supply shocks (adverse weather conditions, El Niño, war in the
Middle East).
ENERGY
Energy. Limited relief, electricity excepted. Re-escalation risks ap -
pear to be rising in the very short term in the Gulf and the Red Sea.
At the same time, the record high level of Gulf oil exports in Sep -
tember (above 80% of pre-war level including bypass and trade shutt-
le) has been enough to stabilize prices at a high level, albeit not to

put significant and lasting downward pressure on crude prices.
- Crude oil: prices declined slightly over the week but remain high
(-3.5% w/w to USD 101.6/b on Monday morning for Brent). The gap
with physical barrel prices (Dated Brent) is rising again USD 24.4/b on
Friday), signalling persisting tensions on the physical market.
- The diesel market got some relief from a G7 decision late on Friday
to release 100 mb of oil and diesel emergency stocks, focused on the

latter. It should take place over the next four months and reportedly
puts off the table US export restrictions. The European benchmark
fell markedly (-12% w/w to USD80/b on Monday morning). However,
market pressure could rise again soon as most Chinese refiners an -
nounced the suspension of fuel exports for October as the government
want to prioritize domestic supply security.
- EU wholesale electricity prices rose sharply in September, reaching

EUR 146/MWh on average, the highest level since Dec 2022.

## Document 307: Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Fed builds credibility with 
hike; one more likely in Dec 
Summary 
Event: The US Federal Reserve hiked its key interest rate by 
25bps to 3.75-4.0%, in a unanimous decision. This marks the 
first hike since 2023, reversing the easing cycle that had 
taken rates down from 4.25% in September 2025 to a trough 
of 3.75% by December 2025. The revised projections 
indicate the Fed expects to raise rates by an additional 25bps 
this year, hold rates in 2027, and cut 25bps in both 2028 and 
2029. It upgraded the long-run rate slightly to 3.2%.  
Market reaction: The US yield curve mildly flattened , while 
the S&P500 index fell 0.5%. The policy rate sensitive US 2 -
year government bond yield jumped 7 bps to 4. 74% as 
markets factored in  three more 25bps hikes by June 2027 . 
The US 10-year yield rose 2bps to 5.02%, its highest since 
2007, while the 30-year yield was little changed. The USD 
index (DXY) rose 0.6%, breaking past its 50- and 100-DMAs. 
Investment strategy: Today’s move realises what we had 
been flagging in recent weeks i.e. the rising risk of a Fed rate 
hike following Fed Chair Warsh’s Jackson Hole speech and 
recent economic data prints, namely: a pick-up in job creation 
and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in oil 
prices due to the re-escalation of the Middle East conflict.  
This hike helps the Fed rebuild credibility as an inflation 
fighter. Given the unanimous decision to hike rates this week, 
we believe there will likely be one more hike before the end 
of this year, and one more in H1 2027 as we expect robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated.  That scenario would still be less 
than the three more rate hikes money markets are pricing 
after the latest Fed move. We believe policy rates can return 
to the 4.0 –4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility of 
cuts in H2 2027  as the impact of tariffs and high oil prices 
start to fade towards the second half of 2027.   
Today’s refreshed Fed policy rate path is mildly bullish for the 
USD index; so we revise up our 3 -month target to 100. We 
also revise up our 3-month 10-year yield target to 5.0-5.25%.  
 The USD rose, while stocks fell after the Fed hiked rates 
and signalled one more rate hike this year; ultra-long 
bonds rose slightly as the Fed rebuild its inflation-
fighting credentials 
Asset performance: 15-Sep close to 16-Sep close

still sees inflation cooling over the next two years
